Universal Brauer–Severi varieties
Frank Gounelas and Daniel Huybrechts

Abstract
. We construct universal Brauer–Severi varieties of fixed period and index and study their geometry. We determine their cohomology and their Brauer and Picard groups and show that they are almost always simply connected. As an application, we reinterpret the discriminant avoidance result of de Jong and Starr in terms of universal Brauer–Severi varieties.



0
0
footnotetext:
Both authors are supported by the ERC Synergy Grant HyperK (ID 854361).

The purpose of this article is to give a concrete
geometric meaning to certain constructions in the literature. The main motivation is to eventually be able to use global methods
in algebraic geometry to approach algebraic problems concerning Brauer groups of function fields.

More concretely, we construct universal Brauer–Severi varieties
{Q}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}
depending on
a fixed degree
n
and period
d
such that any other Brauer–Severi variety with the same invariants
is obtained by pull-back. We phrase this here as follows, see later for more precise versions.

Theorem A
.

Fix three integers
m
,
n
, and
d
with
d\mid n\mid d^{e}
for some
e
.
Then there exists a Brauer–Severi variety
{Q}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}
of relative dimension
n-1
, index
n
, and period
d
over
a simply connected, quasi-projective, smooth variety
{B}
such that

{\rm Pic}({B})\simeq\mathbb{Z}~\text{ and }~{\rm Br}({B})\simeq\lx@nobreakspace\mathbb{Z}/d\mathbb{Z}.

and with the following universality property:
If
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
is any Brauer–Severi variety of relative dimension
n-1
and period dividing
d

over a quasi-projective variety
X
of dimension
\leq m
,
then
P
is isomorphic to the pull-back of
{Q}
under a classifying morphism

X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}
.

All varieties will be considered over a fixed field
k
. The universality of our construction holds without
any further assumption, but the description of the fundamental group and of the Brauer group
assumes
k
to be algebraically closed.

Note that in the above statement,
the classifying map
X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}
is not necessarily unique. Also, there is in fact an ascending sequence
of universal Brauer–Severi varieties
{Q}_{0}\subset{Q}_{1}\subset\cdots
over a sequence
of closed immersions
{B}_{0}\subset{B}_{1}\subset\cdots
. The above result is valid for any

{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
with
i\gg 0
. For the minimal universal Brauer–Severi variety
{Q}_{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{0}

the fundamental group is non-trivial and the Picard group is cyclic of finite order.

Theorem B
.

For
k=\mathbb{C}
the singular cohomology of
{B}_{i}
is given
as

\textstyle{H^{\ast}({B}_{i},\mathbb{Q})\simeq\bigwedge^{\ast}\langle\xi_{2n+1},\ldots,\xi_{2N-1}\rangle\otimes H^{*}({\rm Gr}(N,N+i),\mathbb{Q}).}

Here,
N={n+d-1\choose n-1}
and
\xi_{2j-1}\in H^{2j-1}({B}_{i},\mathbb{Q})
are classes of weight
2j
and type
(j,j)
in the mixed
Hodge structure of
B_{i}
.

Pulling back the classes
\xi_{2j-1}
under the classifying morphism
\psi\colon X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
in Theorem
A

leads to classes in
\psi^{\ast}\xi_{2j-1}\in H^{2j-1}(X,\mathbb{Q})
which we view as obstructions to extend the Brauer–Severi variety

P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
. More precisely, if
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
extends to a Brauer–Severi variety
\bar{P}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\bar{X}

over a smooth complex projective variety
\bar{X}
, then
\psi^{\ast}(\xi_{2j-1})=0
, cf. Remark
3.7
. Ultimately, the computation describes the cohomology of the classifying space of the group that governs Brauer–Severi varieties of relative dimension
n-1
with a fixed relative
{\cal O}(d)
,
cf. Remarks
1.2
&
3.9
. However, for geometric considerations the finite approximations
{B}_{i}
are crucial.

The idea to use universal structures in the algebraic theory of central division algebras is not new,
but the geometric emphasis on Brauer–Severi varieties is. For example, Amitsur
[
1
]
constructed the universal division algebra
{\rm UD}(n,k)
of index
n

over a certain field extension of
k
. The notion has been studied intensely in the algebra literature,
see
[
6
]
for a recent survey. At least morally, there is a link between the universal
Brauer–Severi variety constructed here and
{\rm UD}(n,k)
. More precisely,
the division algebra associated to the generic fibre
{Q}_{\eta}
over the function field

K({B})
is obtained by specialisation of
{\rm UD}(n,k)
. However, we are not aware of a geometric
meaningful interpretation of this link. Variants of the universal division algebra taking into account
also the period have been introduced and studied by Saltman
[
43
,
44
]
, see also
[
47
]
.
The construction of
{Q}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}
lends itself naturally to global considerations.
For example, the
B_{i}
,
i\gg 0
, admit compactifications by projective varieties with a boundary of arbitrarily high codimension which suggests the use of cohomological methods. Theorem
B
is a first step in this direction and we plan to come back to this in a future paper.

To demonstrate the usefulness of the notion of universal Brauer–Severi varieties for geometric
problems, we revisit in §​
4
work of de Jong and Starr. Their article
[
17
]

was in fact the starting point for this paper. To provide some background, recall that the period
{\rm per}(\alpha)

of a Brauer class
\alpha
is simply its order and the index
{\rm ind}(\alpha)
is the degree of the
central division algebra representing
\alpha
(at the generic point). It is known classically that

{\rm per}(\alpha)\mid{\rm ind}(\alpha)\mid{\rm per}(\alpha)^{e_{\alpha}}
for some
e_{\alpha}
and Colliot-Thélène

[
11
]
conjectured that one can choose
e_{\alpha}=\dim(X)-1
for all
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(K(X))
with
X
a variety
over an algebraically closed field. The conjecture has been proved for unramified classes on
projective surfaces by de Jong
[
15
]
with further contributions by de Jong and Starr
[
17
]

and Lieblich
[
33
,
34
]
. Despite some recent results for unramified classes
[
16
,
28
]
, the conjecture is essentially completely open in higher dimensions. However, de Jong and Starr managed to prove that once the conjecture is settled for unramified classes,
so for
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(X)
with
X
projective, it also holds for all ramified classes
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(K(X))
.
Their result, not explicitly stated in
[
17
]
, runs under the name of ‘discriminant avoidance’ and
will be recalled as Theorem
4.1
. We provide a proof that roughly follows the original arguments
in
[
17
]
but in the language of universal Brauer–Severi varieties.

Acknowledgement:
The text grew out of a talk in a seminar on Brauer groups in 2025. The original goal was to present the discriminant avoidance
result of de Jong and Starr
[
17
]
. Looking for alternative ways to present the material, we came
up with the notion of the universal Brauer–Severi variety which not only sheds a different light
on the results in
[
17
]
but turned out to be of independent interest.
We wish to thank the participants of the seminar for interesting questions and feedback.
Special thanks to Giacomo Mezzedimi for the organisation of the seminar. We are also grateful
to Nicolas Perrin for answering a naive question on reductive group quotients
and to Asher Auel for helpful comments on a preliminary version and his generous help with the literature.

1.
Universal Brauer–Severi varieties

In this section we introduce the universal quasi-projective Brauer–Severi variety
Q\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
of index
n
and period
d
. In fact, we construct a series
Q_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B_{i}
,
i\geq 0
, of universal Brauer–Severi varieties contained
in each other and one should think of the limit
Q_{\infty}=\lim Q_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B_{\infty}=\lim B_{i}
as the infinite-dimensional universal
Brauer–Severi variety. The distinguishing property of
Q_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B_{i}
for large
i
is the existence
of a compactification by a projective variety with a boundary of arbitrarily large codimension. Furthermore,
various (cohomological) invariants will turn out to stabilise for large
i
, see §​
3.4
.

The construction is motivated by a result of de Jong and Starr
[
17
, Prop. 2.5.1]
for general reductive groups. Our construction only deals with Brauer–Severi varieties, so the group
{\rm PGL}
,
but is more concrete and potentially more transparent from a geometric point of view.

1.1.
Preparations: period and index

Assume
\pi\colon P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
is a Brauer–Severi variety of relative dimension
n-1
over a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
over a field
k
and let
\alpha=[P]\in{\rm Br}(X)
be its Brauer class.

For the convenience of the reader, we recall two well-known facts
that allow for a geometric way of computing the period and the index
of a Brauer class. We start with the period, cf.
[
5
, §​ 2]
.

Lemma 1.1
.

The period
{\rm per}(\alpha)
divides an integer
d
if and only if there exists a line bundle
{\cal O}(d)

on
P
which when restricted to every geometric fibre
P_{x}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
gives

{\cal O}(d)
. In other words,

{\rm per}(\alpha)=\min\{\,d\mid\exists\,\,{\cal O}(d)\in{\rm Pic}(P)\,\}.

Proof.

For example, one can argue using twisted sheaves. If
P
is viewed as the
projectivisation of an
\alpha
-twisted locally free sheaf
E
, then the direct image of a line bundle
{\cal O}(d)

is untwisted and, up to tensoring with line bundles, isomorphic to the dual of the symmetric
product
S^{d}E
. Hence,
S^{d}E
is untwisted and, therefore,
\alpha^{d}=1
. Conversely,
combining the fact that
{\cal O}(1)
always exists as a
\pi^{\ast}\alpha
-twisted sheaf and
that
\alpha
can be represented by a cocycle with
\alpha_{ijk}^{d}=1
for
d={\rm per}(\alpha)
,
we find that
{\cal O}(d)
always exists as an untwisted sheaf.
∎

Note that the dual of the relative canonical bundle
\omega_{\pi}
is fibrewise just
{\cal O}(-n)
,
which implies the well-known divisibility
{\rm per}(\alpha)\mid n
and, hence,
{\rm per}(\alpha)\mid{\rm ind}(\alpha)
.

Remark 1.2
.

The cohomological version of the lemma uses the short
exact sequence of sheaves in the étale topology (or fppf when the characteristic divides
n
):

(1.1)

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},{\cal O}(d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Here,
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})\simeq{\rm PGL}(n)
and
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},{\cal O}(d))
is the group of
automorphisms of the line bundle
{\cal O}(d)
over
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. For
d=1
the latter is simply

{\rm GL}(n)
and (
1.1
) is the short exact sequence defining
{\rm PGL}(n)
. For
d>1
one
finds
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},{\cal O}(d))\simeq{\rm GL}(n)\,/\,\mu_{d}
and the special case
d=n
leads to
the split group
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},{\cal O}(n))\simeq\mathbb{G}_{m}\times{\rm PGL}(n)
. To see this, use that any automorphism of
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
lifts naturally to an automorphism of the line bundle
{\cal O}(n)\simeq\omega^{\ast}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}
or
that
\det\colon{\rm GL}(n)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}
induces a split of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\simeq\mathbb{G}_{m}\,/\,\mu_{d}\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm GL}(n)\,/\,\mu_{d}
.
Note that for
d\mid n
one also has
{\rm GL}(n)\,/\,\mu_{d}\simeq{\rm SL}(n)\,/\,\mu_{d}
. Cohomological properties of
{\rm SL}(n)\,/\,\mu_{d}
have been investigated in the past, see e.g.
[
8
, §​ 4]
.

Furthermore, sequence (
1.1
) induces the exact cohomology sequence

\textstyle{H^{1}(X,{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},{\cal O}(d)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,{\rm PGL}(n))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and we have that
\delta[P]=d\cdot\alpha
, cf.
[
7
, Ex. 1.1]
.

The Brauer–Severi variety
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
restricted to any open subset
U\subset X
defines a Brauer–Severi variety
P_{U}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces U
over
U
. Similarly, restricting to the generic point
\eta={\rm Spec}(K(X))\in X
defines a Brauer–Severi variety

P_{\eta}
over the function field
K(X)
of
X
. This induces the
natural homomorphism
{\rm Br}(X)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm Br}(K(X))
,
\alpha\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\alpha_{\eta}
, which is injective for locally factorial
X
, cf.
[
14
, Thm. 3.5.5]
. Of course, every class in
{\rm Br}(K(X))
is realised by a Brauer class
and by a Brauer–Severi variety on some open subset
U\subset X
and, in order to argue geometrically, we often fix such a realisation.

Note that in general the index
{\rm ind}(\alpha)
of a Brauer class
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(X)
is not realised as the degree
\deg({\cal A})
of an Azumaya algebra
{\cal A}
representing
\alpha
, see
[
2
]
. Equivalently,
{\rm ind}(\alpha)
can typically not be computed as
\dim(P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X)+1
of any single Brauer–Severi variety
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
representing
\alpha
. This is different for Brauer classes over the function field. Indeed,
the index of a Brauer class over a field is the degree of the unique central division algebra realising the class. Also recall that period and index behave well under the restriction morphisms, i.e.
{\rm ind}(\alpha)={\rm ind}(\alpha_{U})={\rm ind}(\alpha_{\eta})
.

For the proof of the following we refer to
[
5
, §​ 3]
or
[
31
, §​ 8]
.

Lemma 1.3
.

Assume
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(K)
is a Brauer class over a field
K
represented by a Brauer–Severi variety
P
over
K
. Then

{\rm ind}(\alpha)=\min\{\,\dim(P^{\prime})+1\mid P^{\prime}\subset P\text{ linear}\,\}.

∎

The minimum on the right hand side is taken over all
K
-rational linear subspaces. For example, if
P
admits a rational point, which we may view as a zero-dimensional linear subspace
P^{\prime}\subset P
, then

{\rm ind}(\alpha)=1
, i.e.
\alpha\in{\rm Br}(K)
is in fact trivial. Also, by definition of the index, we know that
{\rm ind}(\alpha)\mid\dim(P)+1
, which restricts the possible dimensions of linear subspaces. For example,
P
does not contain a codimension
one linear subspace unless
\alpha
is trivial, which fits well with Lemma

1.1
.

Remark 1.4
.

For a Brauer–Severi variety
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
over a locally factorial variety
X
the lemma can be rephrased geometrically by saying that the index is the minimum of all
n^{\prime}
such that the relative Grassmannian
\text{Gr}(n^{\prime}-1,P)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
admits a rational section, something we will explore in §​
4
.

1.2.
Construction

Let
V
be a vector space of dimension
n
over a field
k
and let

d
and
i
be non-negative integers. In the applications later, we will assume that
d
divides
n
and
that
n\mid d^{e}
for some exponent
e
. One could also just avoid choosing
d
by setting
d=n
, but the results would be less precise.

We introduce the vector spaces
S^{d}_{i}V\coloneqq S^{d}V\oplus k^{\oplus i}

which are of dimension
N_{i}=N_{i}(d,n)={n+d-1\choose n-1}+i
. We often simply write
N=N_{0}
.
The natural inclusions

(1.2)

S^{d}V=S^{d}_{0}V\subset S^{d}_{1}V\subset\cdots

are all of codimension one and we will write

\nu_{d,i}\colon\mathbb{P}(V)\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V)\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)

for the induced composition with the
d
-th Veronese embedding
\nu_{d}=\nu_{d,0}
. We recommend to restrict to the case
i=0
during a first reading.

The image of each of the
\nu_{d,i}
defines a
k
-rational point

[\operatorname{im}(\nu_{d,i})]\in H_{d,i}\coloneqq{\rm Hilb}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V))

in the Hilbert scheme of closed subschemes of
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)
.

The Hilbert scheme
H_{d,i}
comes with the natural action of
{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\simeq{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V))
.
The stabiliser of this action at the point
[\operatorname{im}(\nu_{d,i})]\in H_{d,i}
shall be denoted

{\rm PGL}(V,i)
. In other words,
{\rm PGL}(V,i)\subset{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)
is the subgroup of all elements that restrict to an automorphism of
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V)
, which then is induced by an element in

{\rm PGL}(V)
via the natural embedding
{\rm PGL}(V)\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm PGL}(S^{d}V)
.
In concrete terms,
{\rm PGL}(V,i)
is the quotient
{\rm GL}(V,i)/k^{\ast}
with

{\rm GL}(V,i)=\left(\begin{matrix}{\rm GL}(V)&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}(k^{\oplus i})\end{matrix}\right).

Hence, the orbit
{B}_{i}\subset H_{d,i}
through
[\operatorname{im}(\nu_{d,i})]\in H_{d,i}

is isomorphic to the quotient

(1.3)

\displaystyle{B}_{i}\coloneqq{B}_{i}(d,n)

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\cdot[\operatorname{im}(\nu_{d,i})]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm PGL}(V,i)\simeq{\rm GL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm GL}(V,i).

Note that the last quotient is not a quotient by a subgroup, for

{\rm GL}(V)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm GL}(S^{d}V_{i})
has a non-trivial kernel isomorphic to
\mu_{d}
.
The description of
{B}_{i}
as a quotient allows one to easily compute its dimension as

(1.4)

\dim{B}_{i}=N^{2}+iN-n^{2},

which only depends on the ‘index’
n
, the ‘period’
d
, and the additional integer
i
.

It will turn out that certain features distinguish

{B}_{0}\simeq{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)\,/\,{\rm GL}(V)

from the higher
{B}_{i}
,
i>0
. For one,
{B}_{0}
is affine, see §​
1.3
below, while the higher ones
{B}_{1},{B}_{2},\ldots
are not.

By construction,
{B}_{i}\subset H_{d,i}
is the open subset of the Hilbert scheme that parametrises all closed subschemes of
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)

that are projective equivalent to
\operatorname{im}(\nu_{d,i})\subset\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)
over the algebraic closure of
k
.
The universal family of those is obtained by restricting the universal subscheme
{\cal Z}\subset H_{d,i}\times\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)
to
{B}_{i}\subset H_{d,i}
. We call it the
universal Brauer–Severi variety

\pi_{i}\colon{Q}_{i}\coloneqq{\cal Z}_{{B}_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}.

As a homogeneous space,
{Q}_{i}
is described as the quotient

(1.5)

{Q}_{i}\simeq{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm PGL}(V,i)_{x}\simeq{\rm GL}(S_{i}^{d}V)\,/\,{\rm GL}(V,i)_{x},

and as a homogeneous bundle it can be written as

{Q}_{i}\simeq{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\times_{{\rm PGL}(V)}\mathbb{P}(V).

Here,
{\rm PGL}(V,i)_{x}\subset{\rm PGL}(V,i)
is the stabiliser of a point
x\in\mathbb{P}(V)
of the natural action of
{\rm PGL}(V,i)
on
\mathbb{P}(V)
and, hence,
{\rm PGL}(V,i)_{x}\simeq{\rm GL}(V,i)_{x}\,/\,k^{\ast}
with

(1.6)

{\rm GL}(V,i)_{x}\simeq\left(\begin{matrix}\left(\begin{matrix}k^{\ast}&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}(n-1)\end{matrix}\right)&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}(k^{\oplus i})\end{matrix}\right).

Note that by construction, the universal Brauer–Severi variety
{Q}_{i}
is homogeneous with respect to the action of
{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)
and the projection
{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
is equivariant.

The closed embeddings
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)\subset\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i+1}V)
induce closed embeddings
{B}_{i}\subset{B}_{i+1}
such that the restriction of
{Q}_{i+1}|_{{B}_{i}}
gives back
{Q}_{i}
. In other words, there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{Q}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}}
\textstyle{{Q}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{Q}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{i}}
\textstyle{{Q}_{i+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{i+1}}
\textstyle{{B}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{B}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{B}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{B}_{i+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

1.3.
Compactifications

For
i=0
the base
{B}_{0}
is the quotient of the affine algebraic group

{\rm PGL}(S^{d}V)
by the reductive group
{\rm PGL}(V)
and hence affine. In particular, any compactification

{B}_{0}\subset\bar{B}_{0}
by a projective variety
\bar{B}_{0}
has at least one boundary component of codimension one. However, as
i
grows the situation changes.
To make this precise, we use arguments that go back to

[
48
, Rem. 1.4]
, but see also
[
13
, Lem. 9.2]
,
[
17
, §​ 2.4]
, and
[
19
]
. Our original motivation comes from trying to understand
[
17
, Prop. 2.5.1]

in concrete geometric terms. Our varieties
{B}_{i}
are the analogue of the smooth atlas
X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm BPGL}
constructed by de Jong and Starr which comes with a small compactification
\bar{X}
of
X
, such that every
{\rm Spec}(K)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm BPGL}
lifts to
X
.

Proposition 1.5
.

Let
c>0
be a fixed integer and choose
i
such that

i\geq c-1
. Then,
{B}_{i}\simeq{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm PGL}(V,i)
admits
a projective compactification all of whose boundary components
are of codimension
\geq c
.

Proof.

The key is to reinterpret the quotient (
1.3
)
as the quotient of a linear space by
{\rm GL}(V)
. For this we consider
the space
U\coloneqq{\rm Hom}(S^{d}V,S^{d}_{i}V)
of all linear maps

\psi\colon S^{d}V\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces S^{d}_{i}V
and denote by
U_{N}\subset U
the open set of all injective maps. Clearly,
U_{N}
is homogeneous under
the natural action of
{\rm GL}(S^{d}_{i}V)
. The stabiliser of the inclusion

S^{d}V\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces S^{d}_{i}V=S^{d}V\oplus k^{\oplus i}
is isomorphic to

H\coloneqq\left(\begin{matrix}{\rm id}_{S^{d}V}&\ast\\
0&{\rm GL}(k^{\oplus i})\end{matrix}\right)

and, therefore,
U_{N}\simeq{\rm GL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,H
.
This quotient comes with a further right action of
{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)
which is given by pre-composition.
We will only need the induced action of the smaller subgroup
{\rm GL}(V)\subset{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)
, where we abuse notation and denote by
{\rm GL}(V)
also its image in
{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)
. Since
{\rm GL}(V,i)=H\cdot{\rm GL}(V)
, this leads to the alternative description of
{B}_{i}
as

{B}_{i}\simeq{\rm PGL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm PGL}(V,i)\simeq{\rm GL}(S^{d}_{i}V)\,/\,{\rm GL}(V,i)\simeq U_{N}\,/\,{\rm GL}(V).

Clearly, the above right action of
{\rm GL}(V)
on
U_{N}
extends naturally to a right action

U\times{\rm GL}(V)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces U
, again by pre-composition. The compactification we want to use is provided by the GIT quotient

U^{\text{s}s}/\!/\,{\rm GL}(V)
.

Lemma 1.6
.

We have
U_{N}\subseteq U^{\text{s}s}
.
1
1

1

This should be seen as a classical result in GIT, but we could not find an appropriate reference. We are still debating whether to include a complete proof.

Hence,
{B}_{i}
can be indeed viewed as an open subset of the projective(!) GIT quotient

(1.7)

{B}_{i}\subset\bar{B}_{i}\coloneqq U^{\text{s}s}/\!/\,{\rm GL}(V)=\mathbb{P}(U)^{\text{s}s}/\!/\,{\rm PGL}(V).

To control the dimension of its boundary
\partial{B}_{i}\subset\bar{B}_{i}
, one uses the stratification

U\,\setminus\,U_{N}=\bigsqcup_{\ell=0}^{N-1}U_{\ell}
, where the strata are defined as
U_{\ell}\coloneqq\{\,\psi\mid{\rm rk}(\psi)=\ell\,\}
. The dimension of each stratum is, cf.
[
32
, §​ 7.2.A]
:

\displaystyle\dim(U_{\ell})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim{\rm Hom}(S^{d}V,k^{\oplus\ell})+\dim{\rm Gr}(\ell,S^{d}_{i}V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N\cdot\ell+\ell\cdot(N_{i}-\ell)=\ell\cdot(2N+i-\ell).

We claim that the codimension of the image of
U_{\ell}^{\rm ss}
in
\bar{B}_{i}
is at least
c
.
For
\ell\leq n
we have in fact already
\dim({B}_{i})-\dim(U_{\ell})\geq i+1\geq c
, which is clearly enough.
For
\ell\geq n
we first observe that
U_{\ell}
always contains a point with finite stabiliser.
Indeed, any
\psi\colon S^{d}V\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces S^{d}_{i}V
with
\psi(v_{i}^{d})=v_{i}^{d}
for
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V
a basis,
has a stabiliser that is contained in
\mu_{d}^{n}
and for
\ell\geq n
every
U_{\ell}
contains a
\psi
with this property. This implies that the dimension of the image of
U_{\ell}^{\rm ss}
in
\bar{B}_{i}
can be computed
as
\dim(U_{\ell})-n^{2}
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\dim({B}_{i})-\dim(U_{\ell})+n^{2}\geq i+1\geq c
,
which is easily verified.
∎

The construction in the above proof, due to Totaro
[
48
]
, appears mysterious at first glance, but it can be given a geometric interpretation as follows.
The further quotient by
{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)
leads to a morphism to the Grassmannian

{B}_{i}\simeq U_{N}\,/\,{\rm GL}(V)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces U_{N}\,/\,{\rm GL}(S^{d}V)\simeq{\rm Gr}(N,S^{d}_{i}V),

which maps
[Z]\in{B}_{i}\subset{\rm Hilb}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}))
to its span
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}\simeq\langle Z\rangle\subset\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)
viewed as a point in
{\rm Gr}_{i}\coloneqq{\rm Gr}(N,S^{d}_{i}V)
. Those fit into a commutative diagram of closed immersions

\textstyle{{B}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\textstyle{{B}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{{B}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{{B}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{3}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\text{Spec}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{1}V^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Gr}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Gr}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}

where the embedding
{\rm Gr}_{i}={\rm Gr}(N,S^{d}_{i}V)\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm Gr}_{i+1}={\rm Gr}(N,S^{d}_{i+1}V)
is obtained
by viewing an
N
-dimensional subspace
A\subset S^{d}_{i}V=S^{d}V\oplus k^{\oplus i}
as an
N
-dimensional subspace of
S^{d}_{i+1}V=S^{d}V\oplus k^{\oplus i}\oplus k
.

Example 1.7
.

As a special case, we mention that
{B}_{i}
admits a compactification with a boundary
of codimension at least two as soon as
i\geq 1
.
Note, however, the compactification
{B}_{i}\subset\bar{B}_{i}
constructed above should be expected to be singular (but all singularities are rational, cf.
[
10
]
).

Indeed, at least for
k=\mathbb{C}
,
the quotient
{B}_{i}\simeq U_{N}\,/\!/\,{\rm GL}(V)
is known to be stably rational, see
[
13
, Prop. 4.7]

or
[
9
, Thm. 1.1]
for the quotient by
{\rm SL}(V)
.
If
\bar{B}_{i}
were smooth, then by purity
{\rm Br}({B}_{i})\simeq{\rm Br}(\bar{B}_{i})
, but the
Brauer group of a stably rational smooth projective variety is trivial and we show below that it is
not for
{B}_{i}
.

Example 1.8
.

The easiest case where we can describe
{B}_{0}
fully is the universal conic, i.e. the case
n=d=2
for which
N=3
. The relevant component of the Hilbert scheme is
the linear system
|{\cal O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2)|\simeq\mathbb{P}^{5}
and
{B}_{0}\subset|{\cal O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2)|
is simply the open subset of all smooth conics in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
or, in other words,
{B}_{0}
is the complement of the discriminant
divisor which is a hypersurface
D\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
of degree four. As such,
{B}_{0}
is certainly affine
and
|{\cal O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2)|
should count as its minimal compactification.

Later we will be interested in various invariants of the spaces
{B}_{i}
, cf. Propositions
2.1
,

2.2
, and
2.4
. We remark here already that for
n=d=2
the group
\pi_{1}({B}_{0})
cyclic of order three and not four, as one might expect for the complement of a degree four hypersurface, but
D
is singular. This classical fact is surprisingly hard to find in the literature, but it is covered by the more general results in
[
35
]
.

Remark 1.9
.

In §​
3.3
we will consider the fibres
{B}_{i,x}
of the projection
{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)

over a point
x\in\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)
as a closed subscheme of
{B}_{i}
. The arguments in the above proof
can be adapted to prove that for
i\gg 0
also
{B}_{i,x}
admits a projective compactification with boundary components of arbitrarily high codimension. In fact, it is the closure of
{B}_{i,x}
in the GIT quotient
\mathbb{P}(U)^{\rm ss}\,/\!/\,{\rm PGL}(V)
that provides the desired compactification.

1.4.
Classifying maps I

The universal Brauer–Severi varieties serve various purposes.
For those problems that are concerned with the ramified Brauer group, so the Brauer group
{\rm Br}(K(X))
of the function
field of a variety, the case
i=0
is the most natural one. For more global aspects, the higher

{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
,
i\gg 0
, are more useful.

In the following, we fix again
n
and
d
and
let
{Q}_{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{0}
be the
0
th universal Brauer–Severi variety constructed before.

Proposition 1.10
.

Assume
\pi\colon P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
is a Brauer–Severi variety (with
X
an arbitrary variety) of relative dimension
n-1
with its period dividing the fixed integer
d
. Then there exists a Zariski open covering

X=\bigcup U_{i}
and morphisms
\varphi_{i}\colon U_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{0}
such that
the restriction
P|_{U_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces U_{i}
is isomorphic to the pull-back of
{Q}_{0}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{0}
under
\varphi_{i}
, i.e. there exist isomorphisms

P|_{U_{i}}\simeq\varphi_{i}^{\ast}{Q}_{0}\simeq{Q}_{0}\times_{{B}_{0}}U_{i}

that are compatible with the projections onto
U_{i}
.

Proof.

By virtue of Lemma
1.1
, the period of
P
divides
d
if and only if there exists a line bundle
on
P
which fibrewise is
{\cal O}(d)
on
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. The dual of its (untwisted!) direct image
{\cal E}\coloneqq\pi_{\ast}{\cal O}(d)^{\vee}
is locally free of rank
N

and the relative Veronese map defines a closed embedding

(1.8)

\textstyle{P~\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}({\cal E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

relative over
X
.

Now pick a Zariski open covering
X=\bigcup U_{i}
such that the restriction

{\cal E}_{U_{i}}
is trivial and fix trivialisations
{\cal E}_{U_{i}}\simeq S^{d}V\otimes{\cal O}_{U_{i}}
. Then the restriction of (
1.8
) to
U_{i}
composed
with the induced trivialisation
\mathbb{P}({\cal E}_{U_{i}})\simeq\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V)\times U_{i}
can
be viewed as a flat family
P|_{U_{i}}\subset U_{i}\times\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V)
of closed
subschemes of
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V)
parametrised by
U_{i}
. Let
\varphi_{i}\colon U_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{\rm Hilb}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V))
denote the classifying morphism for this family.
As its image is contained in the
{\rm PGL}(S^{d}V)
-orbit
{B}_{0}\subset{\rm Hilb}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}V))
, we obtain the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{P|_{U_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\times}
\textstyle{{Q}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{B}_{0}.}

This proves the assertion.
∎

The ultimate local version of the proposition is the following function field rephrasing.

Corollary 1.11
.

Assume
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
is a Brauer–Severi variety of relative dimension
n-1
with its period dividing a fixed integer
d
. Then there exists a morphism
\varphi\colon\eta={\rm Spec}(K(X))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{0}
such that
the generic fibre
P_{\eta}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\eta={\rm Spec}(K(X))
is isomorphic to the pull-back
\varphi^{\ast}{Q}_{0}
.∎

Note that the classifying map
\varphi
is not unique, not even up to
the action of
{\rm PGL}(S^{d}V)
.

Remark 1.12
.

The proposition immediately implies upper bounds for the essential dimension of
{\rm PGL}(n)
, which are,
however, weaker than the known ones (which are roughly quadratic in
n
), cf.
[
36
, §​ 10]
or
[
39
, §​ 7]
.

1.5.
Classifying maps II

Let
X
be a quasi-projective variety with an ample line bundle
{\cal L}
and
\pi\colon P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
a Brauer–Severi variety of relative dimension
n-1
. We assume that its period divides a fixed integer
d
, so that we have a relative

{\cal O}(d)
at our disposal and then
{\cal E}\coloneqq\pi_{\ast}{\cal O}(d)^{\vee}
is locally free of rank
N
. Furthermore, for
m\gg 0
there exists a vector space
W
and an injection

(1.9)

{\cal E}\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces W\otimes{\cal L}^{m}

of constant rank.

Remark 1.13
.

We can be more precise about the dimension of
W
. Once we know
that
{\cal E}^{\vee}\otimes{\cal L}^{m}
is globally generated, then there exist
{\rm rk}({\cal E})+\dim(X)=N+\dim(X)
global sections that globally generate
{\cal E}^{\vee}\otimes{\cal L}^{m}
, cf.
[
27
, §​ 5]
. So for this purpose, we may pick
W
to be of dimension
\dim(W)=N+\dim(X)
.

The induced closed embedding

\mathbb{P}({\cal E})\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathbb{P}(W\otimes{\cal L}^{m})\simeq\mathbb{P}(W)\times X

can be composed with (
1.8
) to yield a closed embedding over
X

(1.10)

\textstyle{P~\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(W)\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X.}

Let us now fix an isomorphism
W\simeq S^{d}_{i}V=S^{d}V\oplus k^{\oplus i}
and then view
the smooth family of closed subschemes
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
of
\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)

as the pull-back of the universal
{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
under the classifying map

\psi\colon X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}\subset{\rm Hilb}(\mathbb{P}(S^{d}_{i}V)),

i.e.
P\simeq X\times_{{B}_{i}}{Q}_{i}
.

Altogether we have proved the following global version of Proposition
1.10
.

Proposition 1.14
.

Assume
P\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X
is a Brauer–Severi variety of relative dimension
n-1
with its period dividing a fixed integer
d
. Then for all
i\geq\dim(X)
there exists a morphism
\psi\colon X\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
such that
P
is isomorphic to the pull-back of
the
i
-th universal Brauer–Severi variety
{Q}_{i}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{B}_{i}
, i.e. there exists an isomorphism

P\simeq\psi^{\ast}{Q}_{i}\simeq X\times_{{B}_{i}}{Q}_{i}
compatible with the projections to
X
.∎

Remark 1.15
.

The construction of
\psi
involves a few choices.
Firstly, we had to choose the vector space
W=S^{d}_{i}V
, so essentially its dimension which we tried to keep small depending on the dimension of
X
.

Secondly, we had to choose a surjection
